\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routings constructed in
theorem

6.7
to obtain the columns of the grid.
We start by defining

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq k^{2}-1,

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left((k^{2}-k-1)\cdot\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}}{k}\cdot k!+1\right)}{((k^{2}-k-1)\cdot\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}}{k}\cdot k!+1)}{((k^{2}-k-1)\cdot\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}}{k}\cdot k!+1)}{((k^{2}-k-1)\cdot\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}}{k}\cdot k!+1)}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:temporal one-way connected contains walk with many vertices}}}\mathchoice{\left({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\right)}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})}.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\mathcal{O}(k^{2})
and

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{7}(k))
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Every path of
1
-order-linked sets of width at least
w={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
and length at least
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
contains an acyclic
(k,k)
-grid.

Proof 7.5
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}
be a path of 1-order-linked sets of width at least

\ell\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:order_linked_to_acyclic_grid}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
.
Let

.
By

7.1
, there is an

-

-linkage

of order

in

.
Note that every path in

Let

Since

from time step

to

.
Note that
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T_{i}\right)}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:temporal one-way connected contains walk with many vertices}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,w\right)}{(w,w)}{(w,w)}{(w,w)}

By

6.7
, each

contains a
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

R_{i,j}
corresponds to a
V\mathchoice{\left(\varphi(u_{j})\right)}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}
-
V\mathchoice{\left(\varphi(u_{j+1})\right)}{(\varphi(u_{j+1}))}{(\varphi(u_{j+1}))}{(\varphi(u_{j+1}))}
-path
Q_{i,j,2}
in
D
.
Let
Q_{i,j,1}
be the
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(Q_{i,j-1,2}\right)}{(Q_{i,j-1,2})}{(Q_{i,j-1,2})}{(Q_{i,j-1,2})}
-
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(Q_{i,j,2}\right)}{(Q_{i,j,2})}{(Q_{i,j,2})}{(Q_{i,j,2})}
-path in
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\varphi(u_{j})\right)}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}{(\varphi(u_{j}))}
(to simplify notation, we choose
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(Q_{i,0,2}\right)}{(Q_{i,0,2})}{(Q_{i,0,2})}{(Q_{i,0,2})}
as
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(Q_{i,1,2}\right)}{(Q_{i,1,2})}{(Q_{i,1,2})}{(Q_{i,1,2})}
).

We now set
Q_{i}=Q_{i,1,1}\cdot Q_{i,1,2}\cdot Q_{i,2,1}\cdot Q_{i,2,2}\cdot\ldots\cdot Q_{i,k-1,2}
.
After constructing all
Q_{i}
, set
\mathcal{Q}=\mathchoice{\left(Q_{1},Q_{2},\dots,Q_{k}\right)}{(Q_{1},Q_{2},\dots,Q_{k})}{(Q_{1},Q_{2},\dots,Q_{k})}{(Q_{1},Q_{2},\dots,Q_{k})}
.
Note that the paths in
\mathcal{Q}
are pairwise disjoint.
It is now immediate from the construction that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is an acyclic
(k,k)
-grid.

The previous results show that we can convert an acyclic grid into a path of
r
-order linked sets and vice versa.

7.1
Constructing a path of order-linked sets

We now turn to the problem of actually constructing a path of
r
-order-linked sets in a digraph.
We show first how to construct a path of
1
-order-linked sets from routing temporal digraphs containing
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }

-routings.
Similar to a column in an acyclic grid, such a
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routing allows us to shift one path to its destination without intersecting the other paths in the linkage we construct.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let

be integers.
Let

be the routing temporal digraph of some linkage

through
a sequence
\mathchoice{\left(H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{h}\right)}{(H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{h})}{(H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{h})}{(H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{h})}

{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

-routing on the paths

, ordered according to their occurrence on the

{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routing, then
A
is

-order-linked to

in

\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{h}H_{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{h}H_{i})}{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{h}H_{i})}{(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\cup\bigcup_{i=1}^{h}H_{i})}
, where
A=\{a_{i}\mid a_{i}\text{
is the first vertex of $L_{i}$ on $H_{1}$}\}
and

.

Proof 7.7
.

Let
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
and
B^{\prime}\subseteq B
such that
B^{\prime}
is a 1-shift of
A^{\prime}
.
Let
\pi:A^{\prime}\rightarrow B^{\prime}
be the bijection witnessing that
B
is a 1-shift of
A^{\prime}
.
If
\pi(a_{x})=b_{x}
for all
a_{x}\in A^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
contains an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}

such that
for all
a_{x}\in A^{\prime}
there is an
a_{x}
-
b_{x}
-path in
\mathcal{R}
, and so we are done.

Otherwise, let
x\in\{1,\dots,k\}
be such that
a_{x}\in A^{\prime}
is the unique vertex such that
\pi(a_{x})\neq b_{x}
and let
b_{y}=\pi(a_{x})
.
As
B^{\prime}
is a 1-shift of
A^{\prime}
, we know that
x<y
and
a_{i}\not\in A^{\prime}
for all
i\in\{x+1,\ldots,y-1\}
.

We construct an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}
satisfying
\pi(a_{x})=\mathcal{R}(a_{x})
for all
a_{x}\in A^{\prime}
as follows.
For each
a_{j}\in A^{\prime}
, let
W_{j}
be the temporal walk in the
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.30139pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 8.76 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{6.33235pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 8.76 0)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.29999pt} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.4 0 C 1.23 0.07 0.47 0.45 0 0.87 L 0 -0.87 C 0.47 -0.45 1.23 -0.07 1.4 0 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routing starting in
L_{j}
and ending in
L_{j+1}
and let
W
be the concatenation of
W_{x}\cdot W_{x+1}\cdot\ldots\cdot W_{y-1}
.
The temporal walk
W
connects
L_{x}
to
L_{y}
in
T
, and we can assume it starts at time step
1
and ends at time step
h
.
If
\pi(a_{j})=b_{j}
, we add the path
L_{j}
to
\mathcal{R}
.
Construct
L^{\prime}_{x}
as follows.

Let
(v_{i},t_{i})
and
(v_{j},t_{j})
be two consecutive elements in the sequence of
W
.
We follow
L_{i}
from
H_{t_{i}}
to
H_{t_{j}}
, then take a path
P
in
H_{t_{j}}
connecting
L_{i}
to
L_{j}
.
By construction of
T
and because
a_{n}\not\in A^{\prime}
for all
n\in\{x+1,\ldots,y-1\}
, the path
P
in
H_{t_{j}}
does not intersect any other path of
\mathcal{L}
.
Further, by definition of
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.30139pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 8.76 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{6.33235pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 8.76 0)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.29999pt} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.4 0 C 1.23 0.07 0.47 0.45 0 0.87 L 0 -0.87 C 0.47 -0.45 1.23 -0.07 1.4 0 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routing,
L_{i}
and
L_{j}
only intersect
A^{\prime}
at
a_{x}
or
a_{y}
.
Hence,
L^{\prime}_{x}
is disjoint from all
L_{i}
we chose earlier.
Thus, we obtain an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}
such that
\mathcal{R}(a_{x})=\pi(a_{x})
for all
a_{x}\in A^{\prime}
as desired.

The previous lemma allows us to construct a path of
1
-order-linked sets.
Next, we show that we can increase the order-linkedness of the clusters at the expense of the length of the path of order-linked sets we obtain.
The idea is to “merge” a set of consecutive clusters of a path of
r
-order-linked sets into a single cluster, increasing the order-linkedness.

This idea is much easier to implement in uniform paths of
r
-order-linked sets than in the general case and yields much better bounds.
As the uniform case is sufficient for our application, it is the only case considered here.

The next lemma explains how to construct a single cluster of higher order-linkedness in a path of
r
-order-linked sets by merging the existing clusters.

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
r,c,w
be integers.
Let
D=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}
be a uniform path of
r
-order-linked sets of width
w
and length at least
c-1
.
Then
A(S_{0})
is
cr
-order-linked to
B(S_{\ell})
in
D
.

Proof 7.9
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}\coloneqq\mathscr{P}
.
For each
0\leq i\leq\ell
, let
\varphi_{i}:A(S_{i})\to B(S_{i})
be the bijection witnessing that
A(S_{i})
is
r
-order-linked to
B(S_{i})
.

We define for each
0\leq i\leq\ell
two bijections
\alpha_{i}:A(S_{i})\to\{1,2,\ldots,w\}
and
\beta_{i}:B(S_{i})\to\{1,2,\ldots,w\}
according to
\leq_{A(S_{i})}
and
\leq_{B(S_{i})}
, that is,
\alpha_{i}(a_{j})\leq\alpha_{i}(a_{k})
holds if and only if
a_{j}\leq_{A(S_{i})}a_{k}
holds (and analogously for
\beta_{i}
).
In particular, we have
\varphi_{i}=\beta_{i}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{i}
.
Since
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
is uniform, we also have that
\beta_{i}(b)=\alpha_{i+1}(\mathcal{P}_{i}(b))
for all
0\leq i\leq\ell-1
and all
b\in B(S_{i})
.

Let
A^{\prime}\subseteq A(S_{0})
and let
B^{\prime}\subseteq B(S_{\ell})
be sets of size
k
such that
B^{\prime}
is a
cr
-shift of
A^{\prime}
as witnessed by the bijection
\varphi:A^{\prime}\to B^{\prime}
.
We also define
\pi\coloneqq\beta_{\ell}\circ\varphi\circ\alpha^{-1}_{0}
.

For each
0\leq i\leq c-1
we construct an
A^{\prime}
-
B(S_{i})
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}
of order
|A^{\prime}|
satisfying the following,

(L1)

|\{a\in A^{\prime}\mid\beta_{i}(\mathcal{R}_{i}(a))=\beta_{\ell}(\varphi(a))\}|\geq(i+1)r
.

To simplify notation we set
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{-1})}
as
A^{\prime}
.

On step
i
, let
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{i-1}
be such that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i})}{(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i})}{(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{i-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i-1})}
and let
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}=\mathcal{R}_{i-1}\cdot\mathcal{L}^{1}_{i}
.

Choose the largest possible
A^{\prime\prime}\subseteq A^{\prime}
of size at most
r
by starting at the largest elements of
A^{\prime}
with respect to
\leq_{A(S_{0})}
and proceeding in descending order such that
\alpha_{i}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}(a))\neq\pi(a)
for all
a\in A^{\prime\prime}
.
Let
\widehat{A}=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}(A^{\prime\prime})
.

Let
B^{\prime\prime}=\beta_{i}^{-1}(\pi(\alpha_{i}(\widehat{A}))\cup\beta_{i}^{-1}(\alpha_{i}(\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}\setminus\widehat{A}))
.
Let
\varphi_{i}^{\prime}:\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}\to B^{\prime\prime}
be the bijection defined as follows

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\beta_{i}^{-1}(\pi(\alpha_{i}(a))),&a\in\widehat{A}\\[0.0pt]
\beta_{i}^{-1}(\alpha_{i}(a)),&a\in\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}\setminus\widehat{A}.\end{cases}

Because
A^{\prime\prime}
was constructed by taking the largest elements of
A^{\prime}
with respect to
\leq_{A(S_{0})}
, we have that
\alpha_{i}(a)\not\in\pi(\alpha_{i}(\widehat{A}))
for all
a\in\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}\setminus\widehat{A}
.
Hence, the set
B^{\prime\prime}
is an
r
-shift of
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}
witnessed by
\varphi_{i}^{\prime}
.

Since
A(S_{i})
is
r
-order-linked to
B(S_{i})
in the cluster
S_{i}
, there is a linkage
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{2}
in
S_{i}
such that
\varphi_{i}^{\prime}(\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})})=\mathcal{L}_{i}^{2}(\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\right)}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})}{(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1})})
.
We now set
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{i-1}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{i}^{2}
.

For every
a\in A^{\prime\prime}
we now have
\alpha_{i+1}(\mathcal{R}_{i}(a))=\varphi(a)
.
Since
(L1)
holds for
i-1
, we also have that
(L1)
holds for
i
.

After iterating
c
steps, we have that
\mathcal{R}_{c-1}(A^{\prime})=\varphi(A^{\prime})
since
(L1)
holds for
i=c
and
B^{\prime}
is an
cr
-shift of
A^{\prime}
.
Thus,
\mathcal{R}_{c-1}
is an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage.
Hence,
A(S_{0})
is
cr
-order-linked to
B(S_{\ell})
.

By applying
lemma

7.8
repeatedly, we obtain the following
theorem.

Theorem 7.9
.

Every uniform path of
r
-order-linked sets
D=(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})

.

8
Paths of well-linked sets and fences

The previous section introduces
path of
r
-order-linked sets
as a suitable abstraction of acyclic grids.
We now want to extend this idea to find a similar abstraction of fences as well.

The main difference between a fence and an acyclic grid
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is that if we are interested in routing from left to right, that is, from
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}
to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)}
, then, if
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is an acyclic grid, the
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
is only order-linked to
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
, whereas in a fence these two sets are well-linked.
Consequently, we modify this requirement of the path of
r
-order-linked sets to obtain a suitable abstraction of fences.

Definition 8.1

(path of well-linked sets)
.

A
path of well-linked sets
of width
w
and length
\ell
is a tuple
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
such that

1.

\mathcal{S}
is a sequence of
\ell+1
pairwise disjoint subgraphs
\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell})}
, called
clusters
,

2.

for every
0\leq i\leq\ell
there are disjoint sets
A(S_{i}),B(S_{i})\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\right)}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}
of size
w
such that
A(S_{i})
is well-linked to
B(S_{i})
in
S_{i}
, and

3.

\mathscr{P}
is a sequence of
\ell
pairwise disjoint linkages
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}
such that, for every
0\leq i<\ell
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is a
B(S_{i})
-
A(S_{i+1})
-linkage of order
w
which is internally disjoint from
S_{i}
and
S_{i+1}
and is disjoint from every
S\in\mathcal{S}\setminus\{S_{i},S_{i+1}\}
.

We call
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})

strict
if within each cluster
S_{i}
every vertex
v\in V(S_{i})
occurs on a path from
A(S_{i})
to
B(S_{i})
in
S_{i}
.

As before we define
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})[i,j]\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left((S_{i},\dots,S_{j}),(\mathcal{P}_{i},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{j-1})\right)}{((S_{i},\dots,S_{j}),(\mathcal{P}_{i},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{j-1}))}{((S_{i},\dots,S_{j}),(\mathcal{P}_{i},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{j-1}))}{((S_{i},\dots,S_{j}),(\mathcal{P}_{i},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{j-1}))}
, where
0\leq i\leq j\leq\ell
.

Figure 8
:
A path of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
.
The linkage
\mathcal{L}_{1}
connects all of
A\mathchoice{\left(S_{1}\right)}{(S_{1})}{(S_{1})}{(S_{1})}
to all of
B\mathchoice{\left(S_{1}\right)}{(S_{1})}{(S_{1})}{(S_{1})}
while there are also linkages from every subset of
A\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\right)}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}
to every subset of
B\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}\right)}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}{(S_{i})}
as
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
in
S_{2}
illustrates for example.

While acyclic grids and fences may look quite different, Reed et al. proved
[
32
]
that it is possible to construct a fence from any given acyclic grid.

Lemma 8.2

(
[
32
, statement (4.7)]
)
.

Every acyclic
(pq+1,pq+1)
-grid contains a
(p,q)
-fence.

Next, we show that a similar relation to the one proved in the previous lemma is also true for paths of order-linked sets and paths of well-linked sets.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{proposition:order-linked to path of well-linked sets}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\coloneqq w(\ell+1)
.

†
†
margin:

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{proposition:order-linked to path of well-linked sets}}}

Every path of
w
-order-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}
,
\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})
of width at least
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{proposition:order-linked to path of well-linked sets}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
and length at least
\ell
contains a path of well-linked sets
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell}\right)}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell}\right)}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell}\right)}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\pri of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Further, for every

we have

,

,

and for every

.

Proof 8.4
.

\leq_{A(S_{i})}

.

In the following we construct the sets

iw

for all

and all

.

Finally, choose

and

.

.

\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})}

satisfying the conditions in the statement.

8.1
Paths of well-linked sets contain fences

Next, we show that every path of well-linked sets contains a fence.
Towards this end, we first show that the well-linkedness of
A(S_{i})
to
B(S_{i})
within an individual cluster
S_{i}
can be preserved when going from one cluster to the next, i.e. the set
A(S_{i})
is also well-linked to every
A(S_{j})
and
B(S_{j})
for clusters
S_{j}
with
j>i
appearing later on the path of well-linked sets.

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P}\coloneqq(\mathcal{P}_{0},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}))
be a path of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Then for every
0\leq i<j\leq\ell
, for each
A^{\prime}\in\{A(S_{i}),B(S_{i})\}
and for each
B^{\prime}\in\{B(S_{j}),A(S_{j})\}
we have that
A^{\prime}
is well-linked to
B^{\prime}
in
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[i,j\right]}
.

Proof 8.6
.

We show the case where
A^{\prime}=A(S_{i})
and
B^{\prime}=B(S_{j})
.
The other cases follow analogously.

Let
X\subseteq A^{\prime}
and
Y\subseteq B^{\prime}
be sets of size
k
.
We prove by induction on
j-i
that there is an
X
-
Y
-linkage of order
k
in
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.

If
j-i=1
, then let
B_{i}\subseteq B(S_{i})
be a set of size
k
and let
A_{j}\subseteq A(S_{j})
the set of size
k
such that
\mathcal{P}_{i}(B_{i})=A_{j}
.
Since
A(S_{i})
is well-linked to
B(S_{i})
in
S_{i}
, there is an
A^{\prime}
-
B_{i}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}
of order
k
in
S_{i}
.
Similarly, there is an
A_{j}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{j}
in
S_{j}
.
Let
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{i}
be the paths of
\mathcal{P}_{i}
such that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{i})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i})}{(\mathcal{R}_{i})}
.
Clearly,
\mathcal{R}_{i}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{j}
is an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage of order
k
.

Now consider the case where
j-i>1
.
Choose any subset
B_{i}\subseteq B(S_{i})
of order
|A^{\prime}|=k
.
As before there is an
A^{\prime}
-
B_{i}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{1}
of order
k
in
S_{i}
.
Let
\mathcal{R}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{i}
be the linkage with
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{1})}
.
Note that
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}\subseteq A(S_{i+1})
.
By induction, there is an
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{2}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}{(\mathcal{R}_{2})}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{3}
of order
k
, and so
\mathcal{R}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{R}_{3}
is an
A^{\prime}
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage of order
k
, as desired.

Before we can use
theorem

6.5
to construct our fence, we need to lift the connectivity provided by
H
-routings on digraphs to temporal digraphs.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
T
be a temporal digraph with
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}\geq|V\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}|
and let
H
be a digraph.
If there is a function
\varphi:V\mathchoice{\left(H\right)}{(H)}{(H)}{(H)}\to V\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
which is an
H
-routing for every layer of
T
, then
\varphi
is an
H
-routing for
T
.

Proof 8.8
.

Let
P
be a path in
H
.
Let
u=\varphi(\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)})
and
v=\varphi(\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)})
.
Let
X=\operatorname{Im}(\varphi)\setminus\varphi(V\mathchoice{\left(P\right)}{(P)}{(P)}{(P)})
.
We show that there is a temporal
u
-
v
-path in
T
which is disjoint from
X
.
For each
1\leq i\leq\ell\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
let
V_{i}
be the set of vertices temporally reachable from
u
in at most
i
time steps when starting at time step one without visiting any vertex in
X
.
To simplify notation, let
V_{0}=\emptyset
.
We prove that, if
v\notin V_{i}
, then
V_{i}\neq V_{i-1}
.

For
i=1
the statement is trivial, as
u\in V_{1}\setminus V_{0}
.
Now assume
i>1
.
Let
L
be the
u
-
v
-path witnessing that
\varphi
is an
H
-routing in
D_{i}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
.
If
V\mathchoice{\left(L\right)}{(L)}{(L)}{(L)}\subseteq V_{i-1}
, then we are done and
u
can temporally reach
v
in
T
without intersecting
X
.

Otherwise, as
u\in V_{i-1}
, there is some
w_{1}\in V_{i-1}\setminus V\mathchoice{\left(L\right)}{(L)}{(L)}{(L)}
and some
w_{2}\in V\mathchoice{\left(L\right)}{(L)}{(L)}{(L)}\setminus V_{i-1}
such that
(w_{1},w_{2})\in E\mathchoice{\left(D_{i}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}\right)}{(D_{i}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)})}{(D_{i}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)})}{(D_{i}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)})}
.
Let
R
be a temporal
u
-
w_{1}
-path in
T
ending on time step
i-1
without intersecting
X
.
Then by adding the arc
(w_{1},w_{2})
on time step
i
to
R
, we obtain a temporal
u
-
w_{2}
-path.
Further, as
R
comes from the
H
-routing, it does not contain any vertex from
X
.
Hence,
w_{2}\in V_{i}\setminus V_{i-1}
.

Since
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}\geq|V|
, after
t=|V|
steps we have that
v\in V_{t}
, and so there is a temporal
u
-
v
-path in
T
which does not intersect
X
.

{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

As there are enough clusters, we can find

Theorem 8.8
.

Every path of well-linked sets
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P})}
of width at least
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(p,q\right)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}
and length
\ell\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(p,q\right)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}
contains a
(p,q)
-fence
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}\subseteq A(S_{0})
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}\subseteq B(S_{\ell})
.

Proof 8.9
.

Let
k_{2}=pq+1
,

k_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{n}_{\ref{thm:routing star or path}}}\mathchoice{\left(k_{2},k_{2}\right)}{(k_{2},k_{2})}{(k_{2},k_{2})}{(k_{2},k_{2})}
,

\ell_{7}=k_{1}k_{2}(k_{2}-1)
,

\ell_{6}=\binom{k_{1}}{k_{2}}
,

\ell_{5}=\ell_{6}\ell_{7}
,

\ell_{4}=k_{2}(k_{2}-1)
,

\ell_{3}=\binom{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\cdot(k_{2})!
,

\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}+\ell_{4}
, and

\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}+\ell_{5}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(p,q\right)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}=\ell_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(p,q\right)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}{(p,q)}=k_{1}
.

Let
D=\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\right)}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})})}
.
Let
\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}=\mathcal{S}
and let
\mathcal{L}
be an
A(S_{0})
-
B(S_{\ell})
-linkage of order
k_{1}
within
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.
By
lemma

8.5
, such a linkage exists.

Let
T
be the routing temporal digraph of
\mathcal{L}
through
\mathcal{S}
(see
figure

10
).
Since
A(S_{i})
is well-linked to
B(S_{i})
and every path in
\mathcal{L}
must intersect both
A(S_{i})
and
B(S_{i})
for every
S_{i}\in\mathcal{S}
, we have that every layer
D_{t}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
is strongly connected.

By
theorem

6.5
, every
D_{t}\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
contains a
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

subgraph or a
{\hbox{\hskip 4.50691pt\hskip-4.50691pt\hbox{$\mathbf{K}$}\hskip-4.50691pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 4.50691pt}}_{k_{2}}
-routing
\varphi_{t}
.

We define a concatenation operation for temporal digraphs as follows.
Given two temporal digraphs
T_{1}=(V_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1})
and
T_{2}=(V_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2})
,
we define
T_{1}\cdot T_{2}\coloneqq(V_{1}\cup V_{2},\mathcal{A}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{A}_{2})
.

We show that we can decompose
T
into

T=T_{1,1}\cdot T_{1,2}\cdot\ldots\cdot T_{1,k_{2}-1}\cdot T_{2,1}\cdot\ldots\cdot T_{2,k_{2}-1}\cdot\ldots\cdot T_{k_{2},1}\cdot\ldots\cdot T_{k_{2},k_{2}-1}

in such a way that there exists vertices
u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{k_{2}}\in V\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}
such that, for each
1\leq i\leq k_{2}
and each
1\leq j<k_{2}
, there is a temporal
u_{j}
-
u_{j+1}
-path
R_{i,j}
in
T_{i,j}
which does not intersect any vertex
u_{a}\notin\{u_{j},u_{j+1}\}
.
As
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}\geq\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}+\ell_{5}
, we obtain two cases.

Case 1:
There are
\ell_{2}
layers of
T
which contain some path on
k_{2}
vertices.

There are
\ell_{3}=\binom{k_{1}}{k_{2}}\cdot(k_{2})!
many different choices for the path
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.30139pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 8.76 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{6.33235pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 8.76 0)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.29999pt} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.4 0 C 1.23 0.07 0.47 0.45 0 0.87 L 0 -0.87 C 0.47 -0.45 1.23 -0.07 1.4 0 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk_{2}}
.
As
\ell_{2}=\ell_{3}\cdot\ell_{4}
, there are
\ell_{4}
layers of
T
with exactly the same path
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

.

Let

{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

in

as the desired path
R_{i,j}
.

As

.

8.2
Routing through a path of well-linked sets

We close this section by exhibiting various routing properties of paths of well-linked sets used later.
We first observe the following simple property.

Observation 8.2
.

Let
D
be a digraph and
A,B\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
be sets in
D
such that
A
is well-linked to
B
.
Let
v\in V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
be a vertex on some
A
-
B
-path.
Then there is an
(A\cup\{v\})
-
B
-linkage
\mathcal{L}
of order
|A|
such that
v\in\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{L}\right)}{(\mathcal{L})}{(\mathcal{L})}{(\mathcal{L})}
.

Proof 8.10
.

Let
\mathcal{R}
be some
A
-
B
-linkage of order
|A|
.
Let
P
be some
A
-
B
-path containing
v
and let
P^{\prime}
be the
v
-
B
-subpath of
P
.
Let
P^{\prime\prime}
be the largest subpath of
P^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(P^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(P^{\prime\prime})}{(P^{\prime\prime})}{(P^{\prime\prime})}=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(P^{\prime}\right)}{(P^{\prime})}{(P^{\prime})}{(P^{\prime})}
which is internally disjoint from
\mathcal{R}
and let
R\in\mathcal{R}
be the path of
\mathcal{R}
intersecting
P^{\prime\prime}
.
Finally, let
R^{\prime}
be the
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(P^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(P^{\prime\prime})}{(P^{\prime\prime})}{(P^{\prime\prime})}
-
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(R\right)}{(R)}{(R)}{(R)}
subpath of
R
.
It is now immediate that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\coloneqq(\mathcal{R}\setminus\{R\})\cup\{P^{\prime\prime}\cdot R^{\prime}\}
is a linkage of order
|R|
with
v\in\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}
.

When working with paths of well-linked sets later on, we are often in a situation where we are given two equal-sized sets
X
and
Y
of vertices in a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
and we want to find a linkage connecting
X
to
Y
within
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
.
In the next
lemma
, we identify several cases in which these linkages are guaranteed to exist.
This
lemma
is frequently applied in the next steps of the proof.

Lemma 8.11
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}
be a path of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Let
X,Y\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left((\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})\right)}{((\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}))}{((\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}))}{((\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}))}
such that
|X|=|Y|=k
.
Let
f:X\cup Y\rightarrow\mathds{N}
be a function such that
v\in S_{f\mathchoice{\left(v\right)}{(v)}{(v)}{(v)}}\cup\mathcal{P}_{f\mathchoice{\left(v\right)}{(v)}{(v)}{(v)}}
for all
v\in X\cup Y
.
There is an
X
-
Y
-linkage
\mathcal{L}
in
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
if
f\mathchoice{\left(x\right)}{(x)}{(x)}{(x)}\leq f\mathchoice{\left(y\right)}{(y)}{(y)}{(y)}-2
for all
x\in X
and all
y\in Y
and at least one of the following is true:

(N1)

there are
0\leq i<j\leq\ell
such that

and
Y\subseteq A(S_{j})
,

(N2)

|f\mathchoice{\left(x_{1}\right)}{(x_{1})}{(x_{1})}{(x_{1})}-f\mathchoice{\left(x_{2}\right)}{(x_{2})}{(x_{2})}{(x_{2})}|\geq 2
for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
with
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
and there is some
0\leq i\leq\ell
such that
Y\subseteq A(S_{i})
,

(N3)

|f\mathchoice{\left(y_{1}\right)}{(y_{1})}{(y_{1})}{(y_{1})}-f\mathchoice{\left(y_{2}\right)}{(y_{2})}{(y_{2})}{(y_{2})}|\geq 2
for all
y_{1},y_{2}\in Y
with
y_{1}\neq y_{2}
and there is some
0\leq i\leq\ell
such that
X\subseteq B(S_{i})
, or

(N4)

|f\mathchoice{\left(x_{1}\right)}{(x_{1})}{(x_{1})}{(x_{1})}-f\mathchoice{\left(x_{2}\right)}{(x_{2})}{(x_{2})}{(x_{2})}|\geq 2
for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in X
with
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
and
|f\mathchoice{\left(y_{1}\right)}{(y_{1})}{(y_{1})}{(y_{1})}-f\mathchoice{\left(y_{2}\right)}{(y_{2})}{(y_{2})}{(y_{2})}|\geq 2
for all
y_{1},y_{2}\in Y
with
y_{1}\neq y_{2}
.

Furthermore,
\mathcal{L}
is contained inside
\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\!\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[i,j\right]}{\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}\left[i,j\right]}
where
i
is minimal with
S_{i}
containing a vertex from
X
and
j
is maximal with
S_{j}
containing a vertex from
Y
.

Proof 8.12
.

The case where
(N1)
holds follows directly from
lemma

8.5
.

If
(N2)
holds, we construct an
X
-
A(S_{i-1})
-linkage of order
k
as follows.
We first rename the vertices of
X
such that
x_{r}\in V\mathchoice{\left(S_{r}\right)}{(S_{r})}{(S_{r})}{(S_{r})}
for all
x_{r}\in X
.
For each
x_{r}\in X
, let
k_{r}
be the number of vertices in
X
which appear before
x_{r}
along
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.

By
observation

8.2
, there is an
(A(S_{r})\cup\{x_{r}\})
-
B(S_{r})
-linkage
\mathcal{R}_{r}
of order
k_{r}+1
in
S_{r}
such that
x_{r}\in\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{r}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}
.
If
k_{r}>0
, then by
lemma

8.5
there is an
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{r-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{r-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r-1})}
-
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{r}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r})}
-linkage
\mathcal{L}_{r-1}
of order
k_{r}=|\mathcal{R}_{r}|-1
.

Clearly, the concatenation of all
\mathcal{R}_{r}
and all
\mathcal{L}_{r}
above (in the only order possible) yields an
X
-
B(S_{r^{\prime}})
-linkage of order
|X|
, where
r^{\prime}
is the smallest index such that all vertices of
X
appear before
S_{r^{\prime}}
along
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.
Now, by
lemma

8.5
, we have an
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{R}_{r^{\prime}-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{R}_{r^{\prime}-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r^{\prime}-1})}{(\mathcal{R}_{r^{\prime}-1})}
-
Y
-linkage of order
k
, as desired. The proof for the case where
(N3)
holds is analogous to the one of where
(N2)
holds and so we omit it.

If
(N4)
holds, let
r_{y}
be the smallest index such that
S_{r_{y}}
contains a vertex of
Y
and let
r_{x}
be the largest index such that
S_{r_{x}}
contains a vertex of
X
.

Construct linkages
\mathcal{R}_{r}
and
\mathcal{L}_{r}
as in the proof of the case when
(N2)
holds.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be the linkage obtained by concatenating all
\mathcal{R}_{r}
and all
\mathcal{L}_{r}
(in the only possible order) belonging to the vertices of
X
.
Similarly, let
\mathcal{Y}
be the linkage obtained by concatenating all
\mathcal{R}_{r}
and all
\mathcal{L}_{r}
(in the only possible order) belonging to the vertices of
Y
.

Note that
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}\subseteq B(S_{r_{x}})
and that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Y}\right)}{(\mathcal{Y})}{(\mathcal{Y})}{(\mathcal{Y})}\subseteq A(S_{r_{y}})
.
Hence, by
lemma

8.5
there is an
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}
-
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Y}\right)}{(\mathcal{Y})}{(\mathcal{Y})}{(\mathcal{Y})}
-linkage of order
k
, as desired.

The last statement we prove in this section helps us to deal with a situation in which we already have a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}\coloneqq(S_{0},\dots,S_{\ell}),\mathscr{P})
but we would like to restrict the system so that it “starts” at a specific set
A\subseteq A(S_{0})
and ends at some fixed set
B\subseteq B(S_{\ell})
.

Observation 8.3
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1})})}
be a path of well-linked sets of width at least
w
and length
\ell
.
Let
A_{0}\subseteq A(S_{0})
and
B_{\ell}\subseteq B(S_{\ell})
with
|A(S_{0})|=|B_{\ell}|=w
.
Then,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
contains a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell}\right)}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})})
of width
w
and length
\ell
such that
B(S_{\ell}^{\prime})=B_{\ell}
,
A(S_{0}^{\prime})=A_{0}
,
S^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq S_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq\ell
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{i}
for all
0\leq i<\ell
.

Proof 8.13
.

For each
0\leq i<\ell
choose some
B_{i}\subseteq B(S_{i})
of size
w
and let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{i}
be such that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime})}=B_{i}
.
For each
1\leq i\leq\ell
let
A_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i-1}^{\prime}(B_{i-1})
.

For each
0\leq i\leq\ell
let
S_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq S_{i}
be a maximal subgraph of
S_{i}
such that
A_{i}
is well-linked to
B_{i}
in
S_{i}^{\prime}
and for each
v\in V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
there is some

-
B_{i}
-path

in

containing

.
Clearly, if no such path

exists for some vertex

, then we can remove

from
S_{i}^{\prime}
while preserving the property that
A_{i}
is well-linked to
B_{i}
.
Hence, such a subgraph
S_{i}^{\prime}
exists.
We then set
A(S_{i}^{\prime})\coloneqq A_{i}
and
B(S_{i}^{\prime})\coloneqq B_{i}
.

By construction,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathchoice{\left(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})},\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}\right)}{(\mathchoice{\left(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})},\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})},\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})}{(S_{0}^{\prime},\dots,S_{\ell}^{\prime})},\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{\prime},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}^{\prime})})}
is a path of well-linked sets of width

and length

, as desired.

9
Constructing a path of well-linked sets

We show how to obtain a path of well-linked sets from splits and segmentations by using the results from
section

6
, where we defined the
routing temporal digraph
of a linkage
\mathcal{L}
through a sequence of disjoint digraphs
H_{1},H_{2},\dots,H_{t}
.

In order to construct the routing temporal digraph, the linkage
\mathcal{L}
must intersect all
H_{i}
in an
ordered
fashion.
This means that, if one of the linkages in a web
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
is
ordered
with respect to the other, then we can construct such a routing temporal digraph.
This leads us to the following definition of
ordered web
(see
figure

11
for an example of an ordered web).

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be an
\mathchoice{\left(h,v\right)}{(h,v)}{(h,v)}{(h,v)}
-web.
We say that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is an
ordered web
if there is an ordering of
\mathcal{V}=\mathchoice{\left(V_{1},V_{2},\dots,V_{v}\right)}{(V_{1},V_{2},\dots,V_{v})}{(V_{1},V_{2},\dots,V_{v})}{(V_{1},V_{2},\dots,V_{v})}
for which each path
H\in\mathcal{H}
can be decomposed into
H=H_{1}\cdot H_{2}\cdot\ldots\cdot H_{v}
such that
H_{i}
intersects
V_{j}
if and only if
i=j
.

Figure 11
:
A
(5,3)
-ordered web
(\mathcal{H},\{V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},V_{4},V_{5}\})
.

9.1
From ordered webs

Next, we show how to construct a path of
1
-order-linked sets from an ordered web using the framework of
H
-routings developed in
section

6
.
The statement we present is more general than what we need here,
but we require this stronger version in
[
17
]
.
We start by defining

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq w^{2}-1,

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(w\ell\cdot\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}}{w}\cdot w!+1)\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{theorem:one-way connected temporal digraph contains P_k routing}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}\right)}{(w,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)})}{(w,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)})}{(w,{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)})}-1.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-order-linked-sets-with-forward-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{13}(\ell,w))
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be an ordered
(h,v)
-web where

and

Then
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})
contains a path of
w
-order-linked sets
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}

\blacktriangleright

There is a
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}

Proof 9.3
.

6.7

Combining the previous

allows us to construct a path of well-linked sets from an ordered web.
At a later part of our proof, we need additional information about how the linkage

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-well-linked-sets-with-side-linkage}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-well-linked-sets-with-side-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

Proof 9.5
.

By
lemma

9.2
,
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
contains a path of

-order-linked sets

of width

and length
\ell
.
Further, there is a linkage

of order
\ell+1
and a bijection
\pi^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
such that
A(S_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime})\right)}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}
and
V\mathchoice{\left(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime})\right)}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime}))}\cap V\mathchoice{\left((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
for each
0\leq i\leq\ell
.

By
lemma

8.3
, the path of
w
-order-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})
contains a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}),\allowbreak\mathscr{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell-1}))
of length
\ell
and width

.
Additionally, for each
