we have that
S_{i}\subseteq S_{i}^{\prime}
and that
A(S_{i})\subseteq A(S_{i}^{\prime})
.

Finally, let
\mathcal{X}=\{\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime})\mid 0\leq i\leq\ell\}
and let
\pi:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{X}
be the bijection given by
\pi(S_{i})=\pi^{\prime}(S_{i}^{\prime})
.
It is immediate that
\mathcal{X}
and
\pi
satisfy the desired conditions.

We can manipulate the path of well-linked sets given by
corollary

9.4
above to ensure that the extremities of the path of well-linked sets are contained in the extremities of
\mathcal{H}
.
This is useful when we need the end of the path of well-linked sets to be well-linked to its beginning.

We define

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-well-linked-sets-with-side-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)},

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:ordered-web-to-path-of-well-linked-sets-with-side-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell+4w\right)}{(w,\ell+4w)}{(w,\ell+4w)}{(w,\ell+4w)}.

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\in\mathcal{O}(w^{2}\ell^{2})
and

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{25}(w,\ell))
.

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be an ordered
(h,v)
-web.
If
h\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
and
v\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{state:ordered-web-to-well-linked-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
, then
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
contains a path of well-linked sets
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}
of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Additionally,
A(S_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and
B(S_{\ell})\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
.

Proof 9.7
.

Let
\ell_{1}=\ell+4w
.
By
corollary

9.4
,
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}\right)}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}{(\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})})}
contains a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell_{1}}\right)}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell_{1}})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell_{1}})}{(S^{\prime}_{0},S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{\ell_{1}})},\allowbreak\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{0},\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ell_{1}-1}\right))
of width
w
and length
\ell_{1}
.
Additionally, there is a linkage
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
of order
\ell_{1}+1
and a bijection
\pi:\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
such that
A(S_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime})\right)}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}
and
V\mathchoice{\left(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime})\right)}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}{(\pi(S_{i}^{\prime}))}\cap V\mathchoice{\left((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
for each
0\leq i\leq\ell_{1}
.

We construct a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
of width
w
and length
\ell
as follows.
For each
0\leq i\leq\ell_{1}
, let
X_{i}=\pi(S_{i}^{\prime})
, let
a_{i}
be the first intersection of
X_{i}
with
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
and let
b_{i}
be the last intersection of
X_{i}
with
V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
.
Let
A^{\prime}=\{a_{0},a_{2},\ldots,a_{2(w-1)}\}
and
B^{\prime}=\{b_{\ell_{1}},b_{\ell_{1}-2},\ldots,b_{\ell_{1}-2(w-1)}\}
, let
\mathcal{X}_{A}
be the
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}
-
A^{\prime}
-linkage of order
w
inside
\mathcal{X}
and let
\mathcal{X}_{B}
be the
B^{\prime}
-
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{X}\right)}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}{(\mathcal{X})}
-linkage of order
w
inside
\mathcal{X}
.

By
lemma

8.11
(N2)
, there is an
A^{\prime}
-
A(S_{2w}^{\prime})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}_{A}
of order
w
in
\mathchoice{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\!\left[0,2w\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\!\left[0,2w\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\left[0,2w\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\left[0,2w\right]}
.
And analogously, there is a
B(S_{\ell_{1}-2w}^{\prime})
-
B^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{L}_{B}
of order
w
in
\mathchoice{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\!\left[\ell_{1}-2w,\ell_{1}\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\!\left[\ell_{1}-2w,\ell_{1}\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\left[\ell_{1}-2w,\ell_{1}\right]}{(\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\left[\ell_{1}-2w,\ell_{1}\right]}
by
lemma

8.11
(N3)
.

Since
V\mathchoice{\left(X_{i}\right)}{(X_{i})}{(X_{i})}{(X_{i})}\cap V\mathchoice{\left((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime})\right)}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}{((\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\mathscr{P}^{\prime}))}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(S_{i}^{\prime}\right)}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}{(S_{i}^{\prime})}
for all
0\leq i\leq\ell_{1}
, we have that
\mathcal{Y}_{A}\coloneqq\mathcal{X}_{A}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{A}
is a
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}
-
A(S_{2w}^{\prime})
-linkage of order
w
and
\mathcal{Y}_{B}\coloneqq\mathcal{L}_{B}\cdot\mathcal{X}_{B}
is a
B(S_{\ell_{1}-2w}^{\prime})
-
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}
-linkage of order
w
.

Let
S_{0}=\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{2w}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{A}\right)}{(S_{2w}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{A})}{(S_{2w}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{A})}{(S_{2w}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{A})}
,
A(S_{0})=\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Y}_{A}\right)}{(\mathcal{Y}_{A})}{(\mathcal{Y}_{A})}{(\mathcal{Y}_{A})}
,
B(S_{0})=B(S_{2w}^{\prime})
,
S_{\ell}=\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left(S_{2w+\ell}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{B}\right)}{(S_{2w+\ell}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{B})}{(S_{2w+\ell}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{B})}{(S_{2w+\ell}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{Y}_{B})}
,
A(S_{\ell})=A(S_{2w+\ell}^{\prime})
and
B(S_{\ell})=\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Y}_{B}\right)}{(\mathcal{Y}_{B})}{(\mathcal{Y}_{B})}{(\mathcal{Y}_{B})}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{2w+1}^{\prime},S_{2w+2}^{\prime}\dots,S_{2w+\ell-1}^{\prime},S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{2w+1}^{\prime},S_{2w+2}^{\prime}\dots,S_{2w+\ell-1}^{\prime},S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{2w+1}^{\prime},S_{2w+2}^{\prime}\dots,S_{2w+\ell-1}^{\prime},S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{2w+1}^{\prime},S_{2w+2}^{\prime}\dots,S_{2w+\ell-1}^{\prime},S_{\ell})}
and
\mathscr{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{2w},\allowbreak\mathcal{P}_{2w+1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{2w+\ell-1})
.
Clearly,
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
is a path of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Finally, we have
A(S_{0})\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{L}_{1}\right)}{(\mathcal{L}_{1})}{(\mathcal{L}_{1})}{(\mathcal{L}_{1})}\subseteq\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}
and
B(S_{\ell})\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{L}_{3}\right)}{(\mathcal{L}_{3})}{(\mathcal{L}_{3})}{(\mathcal{L}_{3})}\subseteq\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{V})}
.

The construction from
corollary

9.4
above does not guarantee that the paths in
\mathcal{H}
intersect many clusters of the resulting path of well-linked sets.
The reason is that the layers of the routing temporal digraph constructed from an ordered web are only unilateral and not strongly connected.
Therefore, there is no guarantee that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
are well-linked.
While this does not affect our results,
this property is certainly useful in other contexts and we show how to obtain it in the next subsection.
Moreover, in
[
17
]
we require this stronger property in some of our steps.

9.2
Preserving the horizontal paths in a folded ordered web

The issue mentioned at the end of the last section is unavoidable
when working with ordered webs, as the web might be an acyclic grid,
where it is no longer possible to “come back” to a row once we leave it.
To be able to preserve the paths in the linkage
\mathcal{H}
,
we need each path in
\mathcal{V}
to provide us a possibility of
revisiting a path in
\mathcal{H}
which we might have intersected earlier in the web.
This is achieved by the following definition of
folded web

(see
figure

12
for an illustration of the concept).

Definition 9.8
.

An
(h,v)
-web
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a
folded web
if every
V_{i}\in\mathcal{V}
can be split as
V_{i}^{a}\cdot V_{i}^{b}\coloneqq V_{i}
such that both
V_{i}^{a}
and
V_{i}^{b}
intersect all paths of
\mathcal{H}
.

Figure 12
:
A folded
\mathchoice{\left(5,3\right)}{(5,3)}{(5,3)}{(5,3)}
-web.

Folded ordered webs correspond to splits from
definition

5.1
(X1)
.
The example shown in
figure

13
illustrates the connection between splits and folded ordered webs, which we make precise in the following observation.

Figure 13
:
An example of how a
(3,2)
folded ordered web is obtained from a
(4,3)
-split.

Observation 9.1
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}
be a
\mathchoice{\left(2p,q\right)}{(2p,q)}{(2p,q)}{(2p,q)}
-split of
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}\right)}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}{(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})}
.
Then there is some
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}
containing only subpaths of
\mathcal{P}
such that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}
is a folded ordered
(q,p)
-web.

Proof 9.9
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{2p}\right)}{(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{2p})}{(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{2p})}{(P_{1},P_{2},\dots,P_{2p})}\coloneqq\mathcal{P^{\prime}}
be and ordering of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
witnessing that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}{(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})}
is a
\mathchoice{\left(2p,q\right)}{(2p,q)}{(2p,q)}{(2p,q)}
-split.
For each
1\leq i\leq p
let
P_{i}^{\prime}=P_{2i-1}\cdot e_{2i-1}\cdot P_{2i}
, where
e_{2i-1}
is the arc inside
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that
P_{2i-1}\cdot e_{2i-1}\cdot P_{2i}
is a subpath of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}=\mathchoice{\left(P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2},\dots,P^{\prime}_{p}\right)}{(P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2},\dots,P^{\prime}_{p})}{(P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2},\dots,P^{\prime}_{p})}{(P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2},\dots,P^{\prime}_{p})}
.
Now
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime}\right)}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}{(\mathcal{Q}^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime\prime})}
is a folded ordered
(q,p)
-web, which can be seen by partitioning
P_{i}^{\prime}
into
P_{2i-1}
and
P_{2i}
.

Next, we show that if we construct a path of well-linked sets starting from an ordered web that is also folded, then we can construct the path of well-linked sets in a way that the paths in
\mathcal{H}
are guaranteed to intersect the individual clusters of the resulting path of well-linked sets.
The idea of the construction is similar to the proof of
lemma

9.2
, but now we can use
theorem

6.8
in the construction, which yields the extra properties we need.

We define

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{lemma:routing-implies-well-linked}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)},

\displaystyle\@add@raggedright\marginpar{\sloppy\raggedright{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:routing-implies-well-linked}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}\Big(\ell\binom{{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}}{w}+1\Big).

We observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}\in\mathcal{O}(w^{11})
and

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{1}(\operatorname{poly}^{2}(w,\ell))
.

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
be a folded ordered
(h,v)
-web.
If
h\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}
and
v\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{v}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w,\ell\right)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}{(w,\ell)}
, then
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
contains a path of well-linked sets
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S}=\mathchoice{\left(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell}\right)}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})}{(S_{0},S_{1},\dots,S_{\ell})},\mathscr{P})}
of width
w
and length
\ell
.
Additionally, there is a
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
-
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}\right)}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}{(\mathcal{H})}
-linkage
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{2}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{3}
using only arcs of
\mathcal{H}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is an
A(S_{0})
-
B(S_{\ell})
-linkage of order
w
inside
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{3}
are internally disjoint from
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})}
.

Proof 9.11
.

Assume, without loss of generality, that
h={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}
, as any
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
of size
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}
also satisfies the assumptions of the statement above.

Let
\ell_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:routing-implies-well-linked}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}
, and
\ell_{2}=\ell\binom{h}{w}+1
.
Let
\mathchoice{\left(V_{0},V_{1},\dots,V_{v-1}\right)}{(V_{0},V_{1},\dots,V_{v-1})}{(V_{0},V_{1},\dots,V_{v-1})}{(V_{0},V_{1},\dots,V_{v-1})}
be an ordering of
\mathcal{V}
witnessing that
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a folded ordered web.

For each
1\leq i\leq\ell_{2}
let
\mathcal{H}^{i}
be the maximal linkage inside
\mathcal{H}
such that
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}}\right)}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}})}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}})}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}})}
and
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H}^{i}\right)}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}{(\mathcal{H}^{i})}\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(V_{i\ell_{1}-1}\right)}{(V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}{(V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}{(V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}
.
Additionally, let
T_{i}
be the routing temporal digraph of
\mathcal{H}^{i}
through
\mathcal{V}^{i}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}},\ldots,V_{i\ell_{1}-1}\right)}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}},\ldots,V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}},\ldots,V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}{(V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}},\ldots,V_{i\ell_{1}-1})}
.
Because
\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V}\right)}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}{(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{V})}
is a folded web, for every
0\leq j\leq\ell_{1}-1
and every pair of paths
H_{a}^{i},H_{b}^{i}\in\mathcal{H}^{i}
there is a subpath of
V_{(i-1)\ell_{1}+j}
from
V\mathchoice{\left(H_{a}^{i}\right)}{(H_{a}^{i})}{(H_{a}^{i})}{(H_{a}^{i})}
to
V\mathchoice{\left(H_{b}^{i}\right)}{(H_{b}^{i})}{(H_{b}^{i})}{(H_{b}^{i})}
.
Hence, each layer of
T_{i}
is strongly connected.

By construction,
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T_{i}\right)}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}=\ell_{1}
and by assumption
|V\mathchoice{\left(T_{i}\right)}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}{(T_{i})}|=|\mathcal{H}|={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lemma:folded-web-to-pows}}}\mathchoice{\left(w\right)}{(w)}{(w)}{(w)}
.
By
proposition

6.32
, for every

of order

9.3
Back to finding cycles

We conclude this section by showing that digraphs of large directed treewidth contain a large path of well-linked sets where the last cluster is well-linked to the first.
Here, we use the well-linkedness property to construct our set of pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles.
Later, in
[
17
]
, we use this well-linkedness property to construct the linkage required to
close
the path of well-linked sets into a
cycle of well-linked sets
.

In order to conclude the proof of

1.1
, we need Statement (3.1) from
[

]
, which requires the following functions:

\displaystyle n\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2k^{2}-3k+2,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 8({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(\lceil k/2\rceil\right)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}+n\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}),

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 16k({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(\lceil k/2\rceil\right)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}+n\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})+2k+2q^{\prime}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},

\displaystyle q^{\prime}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(\lceil k/2\rceil\right)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}{(\lceil k/2\rceil)}+2n\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}+1.

See
1.2

Because
[
32
]
does not give explicit upper bounds on the functions
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}
above, we first need to determine these bounds.
To this end, we apply a classical result known as
Master Theorem
, stated below.

Theorem 9.14

(Master Theorem
[
9
,
5
]
)
.

Let
a\geq 1
and
b\geq 1
, let
f
be a function and let
T(n)
be defined on non-negative integers by the recurrence
T(n)=aT(n/b)+f(n)
, where we interpret
n/b
to mean either
\lfloor n/b\rfloor
or
\lceil n/b\rceil
.
Then
T(n)
has the following asymptotic bounds.

(M1)

If
f(n)\in O(n^{\log_{b}a-\epsilon})
for some constant
\epsilon>0
, then
T(n)=\Theta(n^{\log_{b}a})
.

(M2)

If
f(n)\in\Theta(n^{\log_{b}a})
, then
T(n)\in\Theta(n^{\log_{b}a}\log n)
.

(M3)

If
f(n)\in\Omega(n^{\log_{b}a+\epsilon})
for some constant
\epsilon>0
, and if
af(n/b)\leq cf(n)
for some constant
c<1
and all sufficiently large
n
, then
T(n)\in\Theta(f(n))
.

It is fairly straightforward to apply
theorem

9.3
in order to obtain polynomial bounds for
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}
.

Observation 9.4
.

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\Theta(k^{3})
and

{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\Theta(k^{3})
.

Proof 9.15
.

We apply
theorem

9.3
.

We rewrite
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
in the form
T_{1}(k)=a_{1}T_{1}(k/b_{1})+f_{1}(k)
by defining
a_{1}=8=2^{3}
,
b_{1}=2
and
f_{1}(k)=8n\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in O(k^{2})
.
From
theorem

9.3
(M1)
we thus obtain that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\Theta(k^{3})
.

Also, we rewrite
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
in the form
T_{2}(k)=a_{2}T_{2}(k/b_{2})+f_{2}(k)
by defining
a_{2}=2
,
b_{2}=2
and
f_{2}(k)=2k^{3}+10k^{2}-13k+12\in O(k^{3})
.
In order to apply
theorem

9.3
(M3)
, we first show that
a_{2}f_{2}(k/b_{2})<cf_{2}(k)
holds for some
c<1
and for all sufficiently large
k
.

\displaystyle a_{2}f_{2}(k/b_{2})

\displaystyle{}<cf_{2}(k)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle 2f_{2}(k/2)-cf_{2}(k)

\displaystyle{}<0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle k^{3}/2+5k^{2}-13k+24-c(2k^{3}+10k^{2}-13k+12)

\displaystyle{}<0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle k^{3}/2+5k^{2}-13k+24-c2k^{3}-c10k^{2}+c13k-c12

\displaystyle{}<0

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(1/2-2c)k^{3}+(5-10c)k^{2}+(13c-13)k-12c+24

\displaystyle{}<0

The inequality above holds, for example, for
c=1/2
and all
k\geq 3
.
By applying
theorem

9.3
(M3)
, we conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\Theta(k^{3})
.

We split the proof of
theorem

1.1
into two cases.
The case where the given digraph has a large directed treewidth can be handled using the statements proven so far.

We define

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{thm:high_dtw_to_POSS_plus_back-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\right)}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\right)}{({\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)},{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoi nkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)})})}.

Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{8}(\operatorname{poly}^{43}(k))
.

Lemma 9.16
.

Let
D
be a digraph with
\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
.
Then,
D
contains
k
pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles.

Proof 9.17
.

Let

q={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{q}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
,

r={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{r}_{\ref{statement:fence-plus-back-linkage-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
,

w_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{w}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(r,q\right)}{(r,q)}{(r,q)}{(r,q)}
,

\ell_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{\ell}_{\ref{thm:poss-to-fence}}}\mathchoice{\left(r,q\right)}{(r,q)}{(r,q)}{(r,q)}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{thm:high_dtw_to_POSS_plus_back-linkage}}}\mathchoice{\left(w_{1},\ell_{1}\right)}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}{(w_{1},\ell_{1})}
.

By
theorem

9.2
,
D
contains a path of well-linked sets
(\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell_{1}}),\mathscr{P})
of width
w_{1}
and length
\ell_{1}
.
Moreover,
B(S_{\ell_{1}})
is well-linked to
A(S_{0})
.

By
theorem

8.1
,
the digraph
\mathsf{D}\mathchoice{\left((\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell_{1}}),\mathscr{P})\right)}{((\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell_{1}}),\mathscr{P}))}{((\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell_{1}}),\mathscr{P}))}{((\mathcal{S}=(S_{0},S_{1},\ldots,S_{\ell_{1}}),\mathscr{P}))}

contains an
(r,q)
-fence
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})

where
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}\allowbreak\subseteq A(S_{0})
and

\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}\subseteq B(S_{\ell_{1}})
.
Hence,
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
is well-linked to
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
.
Because
|\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}|=r
,
there is an
\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
-
\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left(\mathcal{P}\right)}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}{(\mathcal{P})}
-linkage
\mathcal{R}
of order
r
in
D
.

By
lemma

1.2
,
D
contains
k
pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles inside
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
and
\mathcal{R}
.

For the case where the digraph has small directed treewidth, we use the following result due to
[
3
]
.

Lemma
1.3

(
[
3
, Lemma 4.2]
)
.

Let
D
be a digraph with
\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}\leq w
. For each strongly connected digraph
H
, the digraph
D
either has
k
disjoint copies of
H
as a topological minor, or contains a set
T
of at most
k\cdot(w+1)
vertices such that
H
is not a topological minor of
D-T
.

Combining
Lemmas

1.3
and
9.16
yields our final main result.

We define
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{f}_{\ref{statement:elementary-younger}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\coloneqq{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}k
and observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{f}_{\ref{statement:elementary-younger}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}\in\operatorname{exp}_{2}^{8}(\operatorname{poly}^{43}(k))
.

See
1.1

Proof 9.18
.

Let
t_{1}={\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{t}_{\ref{statement:high-dtw-implies-cycles}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
.

Let
H={\hbox{\hskip 4.15276pt\hskip-4.15276pt\hbox{$\mathbf{C}$}\hskip-4.15276pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 4.15276pt}}_{\hskip-0.3014pt2}
.
If
\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}\leq t_{1}-1
, then by
lemma

1.3

D
contains
k
vertex-disjoint copies of
H
as a topological minor or
D
contains a set
X\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
of size at most
kt_{1}
such that
D-X
does not contain
H
as a topological minor.
By choice of
H
, both cases imply the initial statement, and we are done.

Otherwise,
\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}\geq t_{1}
.
By
lemma

9.16
,
D
contains
k
pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles.

10
Conclusion

While we considerably improve the function for Younger’s conjecture, our bounds are likely not tight.
Indeed, the only known lower bound for our function
{\color[rgb]{0.05,0.375,0.0375}\mathsf{f}_{\ref{statement:elementary-younger}}}\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
is
\Omega(k\log k)
, noted by Alon (unpublished, see
[
32
]
, but possibly uses the same techniques as the lower bound for the undirected case due to
[
13
]
).
It is thus natural to ask how far this function can be improved further.

Question 10.1
.

Is there a polynomial
p
such that, for every digraph
D
, there are
k
pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles in
D
or there is some
X\subseteq V\mathchoice{\left(D\right)}{(D)}{(D)}{(D)}
of size at most
p\mathchoice{\left(k\right)}{(k)}{(k)}{(k)}
such that
D-X
is acyclic?

Similarly, the framework we introduce based on
H
-routings, temporal digraphs and paths of well-linked sets is still not fully studied.
In particular, can we improve
theorem

6.7
?
How large do temporal digraphs need to be to certainly contain some
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.30139pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 8.76 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{6.33235pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 8.76 0)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=miter} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.29999pt} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.4 0 C 1.23 0.07 0.47 0.45 0 0.87 L 0 -0.87 C 0.47 -0.45 1.23 -0.07 1.4 0 Z}{} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}\hskip-3.99187pt\hskip 3.99187pt}}}_{\hskip-1.20552ptk}
-routing?

Question 10.2
.

Are there polynomials
p_{1},p_{2}
such that every temporal digraph
T
with
\ell\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}\geq p_{1}(k)
,
|V\mathchoice{\left(T\right)}{(T)}{(T)}{(T)}|\geq p_{2}(k)
and where each layer is unilateral and contains a
{{\hbox{\hskip 3.99187pt\hskip-3.93053pt\hbox{$\mathbf{P}$}\hskip-3.93053pt\hskip-3.99187pt\raisebox{7.7222pt}{\hbox{$\hbox to7.98pt{\vbox to2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.1507pt\lower-1.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}

-routing?

While acyclic grids and fences contain paths of 1-order-linked sets and paths of well-linked sets, respectively, of roughly the same size, the bounds given by
Theorems

7.2
and
8.1
are very large.
The proofs provided here, however, use the pigeon-hole principle, which tends to be very wasteful.
It seems, thus, plausible that those bounds could be improved.

Question 10.3
.

Are there polynomials
p_{1},p_{2}
such that every uniform path of
p_{1}(w)
-order-linked sets of and width
p_{1}(w)
and length
p_{2}(w)
contains a
(w,w)
-acyclic-grid?

Question 10.4
.

Are there polynomials
p_{1},p_{2}
such that every path of well-linked sets of width
p_{1}(w)
and length
p_{2}(w)
contains a
(w,w)
-fence?

Finally, we did not consider any questions relating to the computability of
H
-routings in this paper.
While it is simple to verify that deciding whether a (temporal) digraph contains an
H
-routing is in
NP
, we do not have an
NP
-hardness reduction.

Question 10.5
.

Can we decide in polynomial time if a (temporal) digraph
D
contains an
H
-routing?

Another natural question is how much the framework above helps to obtain better bounds for the Directed Grid Theorem.
At first glance, the path of well-linked sets we obtained may seem to be very close to a cylindrical grid.
Indeed, we can find both a fence and many pairwise vertex-disjoint cycles in a path of well-linked sets.
Alas, the cycles we find may be short and interact poorly with the fence.

The main issue is that, while the end of the path of well-linked sets is itself well-linked to the beginning, the linkages we obtain this way might intersect the path of well-linked sets in arbitrary ways.
While this may quickly lead to many pairwise disjoint cycles if the linkage is going backwards along the path of well-linked sets, these cycles are not “concentric” in the way required for the cylindrical grid.

In order to obtain the cylindrical grid, we need to get a “back-linkage” from the end of the path of well-linked sets back to its beginning which is internally disjoint from said path of well-linked sets.
Formally, we obtain an object which we call a
cycle of well-linked sets
(of width
w
and length
\ell
), which is a pair
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P}\cup\{\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
where
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
is a path of well-linked sets of width
w
and length
\ell-1
, and
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}
is a linkage from the
B
-set of the last cluster to the
A
-set of the first cluster that is internally disjoint from
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{P})
.

As the proof that we can indeed obtain such a back-linkage (and hence, a cycle of well-linked sets) is not trivial and requires several additional steps, we defer it to
[
17
]
.
